- **pyldraw3**: Python package (import name `ldraw`) for reading, writing, building and analysing LDraw LEGO CAD files (.ldr, .mpd, .dat). Covers installation, library setup, the core objects (Piece, Group, Matrix, Vector, Colour, Model), reading models and the map to task subskills. Use when code imports `ldraw` or `ldraw.library.*`, depends on pyldraw3, parses or writes LDraw files, or needs LDraw parts, colours, geometry, connections or the `ldraw` CLI. ([anteloc/iskillz](https://github.com/anteloc/iskillz/tree/HEAD/examples/pyldraw3))
- **pyleoclim**: Analyse paleoclimate proxy time series with Pyleoclim, including irregular sampling, spectra and comparisons across uncertain chronologies. Use for proxy Series and EnsembleSeries analysis, spectral peaks and age-model sensitivity; use xarray for gridded climate fields. ([SteadfastAsArt/geoscience-skills](https://github.com/SteadfastAsArt/geoscience-skills/tree/HEAD/pyleoclim) · ★84)
- **pylgen**: Use when writing or debugging code that uses PyLGEN (`pylgen-core` on PyPI, `import pylgen`), a Python-native compiler/interpreter framework — regex→DFA lexer, attributed grammars with `+=`, LALR(1)/SLR/LR(1) parser generation, AST + visitor framework, optional HTML visualization, Cython acceleration. Triggers on "pylgen", "pylgen-core", "AttributedGrammar", "ParserBuilder", "ASTListView", "IdentedLexer", or building a lexer/parser/DSL/interpreter in Python with this library. ([arpieb/pylgen-skill](https://github.com/arpieb/pylgen-skill/blob/HEAD/SKILL.md))
- **pylhe**: Use when reading or writing Les Houches Event XML (LHE) or HDF5 (LHEH5) files in Python: reading parton-level events from MadGraph, Powheg, Sherpa, Pythia, or Whizard generators, iterating over initial- and final-state parton four-vectors, accessing event weights and scale/PDF reweighting blocks, counting events efficiently, writing modified LHE files back to disk, visualising event topologies with graphviz, or converting events to awkward arrays for vectorized analysis. Also use when the user mentions `LHEFile`, `LHEEvent`, `LHEParticle`, or parsing `.lhe` / `.lhe.gz` / `lhe.hdf5` / `.lheh5`… ([usatlas/marketplace](https://github.com/usatlas/marketplace/tree/HEAD/plugins/atlas/skills/pylhe) · ★6)
- **pylhe**: Use when the user needs to read or inspect Les Houches Event (LHE) files in Python — parsing generator-level events, event weights, particle records, run metadata — with the Scikit-HEP `pylhe` package. Does NOT cover HepMC3 event records (use `pyhepmc`), detector-level ROOT ntuples (use `physlite-basics`), or running a Monte Carlo generator. Disambiguator phrase: pylhe Les Houches Event reader. ([Soap2G/lumi-assistant](https://github.com/Soap2G/lumi-assistant/tree/HEAD/config/skills/access/pylhe) · ★5)
- **pylhe**: Use when reading or writing Les Houches Event (LHE) files in Python: reading parton-level events from MadGraph, Powheg, Sherpa, Pythia, or Whizard generators, iterating over initial- and final-state parton four-vectors, accessing event weights and scale/PDF reweighting blocks, counting events efficiently, writing modified LHE files back to disk, visualising event topologies with graphviz, or converting events to awkward arrays for vectorized analysis. Also use when the user mentions `LHEFile`, `LHEEvent`, `LHEParticle`, or parsing `.lhe` / `.lhe.gz` files. ([MohamedElashri/lhcb-marketplace](https://github.com/MohamedElashri/lhcb-marketplace/tree/HEAD/plugins/hep-tools/skills/pylhe))
- **pylib-agent-benchmarking**: Use when benchmarking opencode agents on Python-library usage tasks, especially to measure whether agent-facing docs or skills improve correctness, tool use, and token efficiency. ([mylonasc/tf_gnns](https://github.com/mylonasc/tf_gnns/tree/HEAD/.opencode/skills/pylib-agent-benchmarking) · ★12)
- **pylib-user-skill**: Use when shipping a user-assistant skill with a Python library so opencode assistants learn the public API from runnable examples, paged retrieval, and an auto-generated API index. ([mylonasc/tf_gnns](https://github.com/mylonasc/tf_gnns/tree/HEAD/.opencode/skills/pylib-user-skill) · ★12)
- **pylibp2p**: Master router and architectural reference for developing, debugging, and maintaining the py-libp2p repository. Guides py-libp2p PR pre-flight validation, newsfragments, Trio structured concurrency auditing, protocol scaffolding, multistream-select, Noise security handshakes, Yamux stream muxing, and interop testing.
